§ 656.10
Application filing requirements.
Except as otherwise permitted by statute or this subpart, an employer who desires to hire an alien for permanent employment in the United States must apply and obtain a labor certification from the Secretary by filing an Application for Permanent Employment Certification with the NPC designated by the OFLC Administrator. This section provides the procedures employers must follow when filing.
(a) What to file.
(1) An employer that desires to apply for permanent labor certification of an immigrant worker must file a complete Application for Permanent Employment Certification, including a valid PWD as required by § 656.40, the employer's legal name, trade name(s), and valid FEIN as well as a valid place of business (physical location) in the United States and a means by which it may be contacted by prospective U.S. applicants for employment;
(2) A copy of the NOF providing notice to employees located at the place(s) of employment, as set forth in § 656.14, must be submitted with the Application for Permanent Employment Certification;
(3) A copy of all records and documentation demonstrating compliance with pre-filing recruitment requirements set forth in § 656.17 or § 656.18, must be submitted with the Application for Permanent Employment Certification, unless the Application for Permanent Employment Certification is subject to supervised recruitment procedures as set forth in § 656.21; and
(4) A recruitment report meeting the requirements set forth in § 656.17(f), and copies of all resumes and/or applications for each U.S. worker who applied for the job opportunity, must be submitted with the Application for Permanent Employment Certification, unless the Application for Permanent Employment Certification is subject to supervised recruitment procedures as set forth in § 656.21.
(b) Timeliness. A complete Application for Permanent Employment Certification, including all supporting documentation and information required at the time of filing under this subpart, must be filed within 30 calendar days after the completion of the recruitment procedures set forth in § 656.17 or the optional pre-filing recruitment procedures set forth in § 656.18.
(c) Location and method of filing.
(1) Electronic filing. The employer must file the Application for Permanent Employment Certification and all required supporting documentation with the NPC using the electronic method(s) designated by the OFLC Administrator. The NPC will return without review any application submitted using a method other than the designated electronic method(s), unless the employer submits the application in accordance with paragraph (c)(2) or (3) of this section.
(2) Filing by mail. Employers that lack adequate access to electronic filing may file the application by mail. The employer must indicate that it is filing by mail due to lack of adequate access to electronic filing. The OFLC Administrator will identify the address to which such filing must be mailed by public notice(s) and by instructions on DOL's website.
(3) Reasonable accommodation. Employers who are unable or limited in their ability to use and/or access the electronic Application for Permanent Employment Certification, or any other form or documentation required under this part, as a result of a disability may request a reasonable accommodation to enable them to participate in the permanent labor certification program. An employer in need of such an accommodation may contact the NPC in writing to the address designated in a notice published in the Federal Register , or for individuals with hearing or speech impairments, 1-877-889-5627 (this is the TTY toll-free Federal Information Relay Service number) for assistance in using, accessing, or filing any form or documentation required under this part, including the Application for Permanent Employment Certification. All requests for an accommodation must include the employer's name, a detailed description of the accommodation needed, and the preferred method of contact. The NPC will respond to the request for a reasonable accommodation within 10 business days of the date of receipt.
(d) Original signature. The Application for Permanent Employment Certification must contain an electronic (scanned) copy of the original signature of the employer (and that of the employer's authorized attorney or agent if the employer is represented by an attorney or agent) or a verifiable electronic signature method, as directed by the OFLC Administrator. If submitted by mail, the Application for Permanent Employment Certification must bear the original signature of the employer and, if applicable, the employer's authorized attorney or agent.
(e) Additional documentation for certain job opportunities. An employer who desires to hire an alien for permanent employment in the United States in one of the following job opportunities must maintain, at the time of filing, the additional documentation required by this section and be prepared to submit such documentation in the ( printed page 74008) event of an audit or in response to a request from the CO:
(1) For a live-in household domestic service worker job opportunity, an employer must maintain the following documentation:
(i) A copy of the executed contract signed and dated by the employer and alien containing, at a minimum, the same material terms of employment specified on the Application for Permanent Employment Certification and the following information and employment guarantees:
(A) A description of the household living accommodations, including the type of residence ( e.g., house or apartment), number of rooms inside the residence, and number of adults and children, with ages of the children, residing in the residence;
(B) The total hours of employment per week and the expected hours of employment per day;
(C) The total amount of any money that the employer will advance to the alien, including a detailed description of each specific item and amount of advance, and the terms of repayment by the alien of any such advance;
(D) A statement that a private room on the employer's premises and board will be provided, at no cost, to the alien;
(E) A statement that the alien is free to leave the employer's premises during all non-work hours, except the alien may work overtime if paid for the overtime at no less than the legally required hourly rate;
(F) A statement that the alien will not be required to give more than two weeks' notice of intent to leave the employment contracted for and the employer must give the alien at least two weeks' notice before terminating employment; and
(G) Any other agreement or material term or condition of employment not specified on the Application for Permanent Employment Certification.
(ii) Documentation that the alien possesses at least one year of full-time paid work experience, exclusive of any training, in the form of written statements, signed and dated from past or present employers, providing a detailed statement of the duties performed and equipment used, identifying the dates (month and year) employment started and ended, hours of work per day, number of days worked per week, place(s) where the alien was employed and the amount of wages paid per week or month.
(2) For a job opportunity with an employer that is a closely held corporation or partnership in which the alien has an ownership interest, or if there is a familial relationship between the stockholders, corporate officers, incorporators, or partners, and the alien, or if the alien is one of a small number of employees, the employer must maintain the following documentation:
(i) A copy of the articles of incorporation, partnership agreement, business license or similar documents that establish the business entity;
(ii) A list of all corporate/company officers and shareholders/partners of the corporation/firm/business, their titles and positions in the business' structure, and a description of the relationships to each other and to the alien;
(iii) The financial history of the corporation/company/partnership, including the total investment in the business entity and the amount of investment of each officer, incorporator/partner and the alien; and
(iv) The name of the business' official with primary responsibility for interviewing and hiring applicants for positions within the organization and the name(s) of the business' official(s) having control or influence over hiring decisions involving the position for which permanent labor certification is sought.
(v) If the alien is one of 10 or fewer employees, the employer must document any familial relationship between the employees and the alien.
(3) For a professional athlete or professional sports team coach job opportunity that meets the definition set forth in § 656.3, the employer must maintain the following documentation:
(i) A copy of the executed written contract, signed and dated by the employer and alien, covering the entire year or period that indicates the specific payment(s) made to the alien;
(ii) An employment letter stating that the alien will work a minimum of ten (10) hours per week during the off-season period; and
(iii) For an alien being sponsored for permanent employment as a professional athlete, a copy of a letter or response, whether electronic or print, from the Players' Association or applicable trade union(s) stating that it was provided notice from the employer, as required by § 656.14(a)(1), and has no objection to the filing of the Application for Permanent Employment Certification.
(f) Filing date.
(1) The filing date for any Application for Permanent Employment Certification received using the electronic method(s) designated by the OFLC Administrator will be the date on which the complete application, including all applicable appendices and supporting documentation, is submitted and accepted for processing with the NPC. The filing date will be based on the time of submission in the Eastern time zone of the United States.
(2) The filing date for any Application for Permanent Employment Certification received in accordance with paragraph (c)(2) or (3) of this section will be the postmark date affixed to the employer's Application for Permanent Employment Certification submission, provided that the NPC determines the application is complete.
(g) Completeness. The employer must file a complete Application for Permanent Employment Certification that meets all the requirements of this part. If an employer submits an incomplete Application for Permanent Employment Certification, it will be returned without review and will not retain a filing date. If an incomplete Application for Permanent Employment Certification is returned unprocessed, the decision will be a final action and will not be subject to reconsideration or review under § 656.26.
(h) Record retention requirements. Copies of Applications for Permanent Employment Certification filed with DOL and all supporting documentation and records demonstrating compliance with this part must be retained by the employer for a period of 5 years from the latest of the following dates: the date on which the Immigrant Petition is approved by USCIS; the date the approved Application for Permanent Employment Certification expires, if the employer does not file an Immigrant Petition using the certification; or the date the Application for Permanent Employment Certification or Immigrant Petition is denied or withdrawn.
§ 656.11
Substitutions and modifications to applications.
(a) Substitution or change to the identity of an alien on any Application for Permanent Employment Certification and on any resulting permanent labor certification is prohibited.
(b) Requests for modifications to any Application for Permanent Employment Certification filed with the NPC and on any resulting permanent labor certification are prohibited.
§ 656.12
Improper commerce and payment.
The following provision applies to any Application for Permanent Employment Certification and permanent labor certification issued under this part.
(a) Applications for Permanent Employment Certification and permanent labor certifications issued under this part are not articles of commerce. They may not be offered for ( printed page 74009) sale, barter or purchase by individuals or entities. Any evidence that an Application for Permanent Employment Certification or a permanent labor certification has been sold, bartered, or purchased will be grounds for investigation under this part and is grounds for denial under § 656.24, revocation under § 656.32, debarment under § 656.31(f), or any combination thereof.
(b) An employer must not seek or receive payment of any kind for any activity related to obtaining permanent labor certification, including payment of the employer's attorneys' fees, whether as an incentive or inducement to filing, or as a reimbursement for costs incurred in preparing or filing an Application for Permanent Employment Certification, except when work to be performed by the alien in connection with the job opportunity would benefit or accrue to the person or entity making the payment, based on that person's or entity's established business relationship with the employer. An alien may pay his or her own costs in connection with a permanent labor certification, including attorneys' fees for representation of the alien, except that where the same attorney represents both the alien and the employer, such costs must be borne by the employer. For purposes of this paragraph (b), payment includes, but is not limited to, monetary payments; wage concessions, including deductions from wages, salary, or benefits; kickbacks, bribes, or tributes; in kind payments; and free labor.
(c) Evidence that an employer has sought or received payment from any source in connection with an Application for Permanent Employment Certificatio n or a permanent labor certification, except for a third party to whose benefit work to be performed in connection with the job opportunity would accrue, based on that person's or entity's established business relationship with the employer, will be grounds for investigation under this part or any appropriate Government agency's procedures, and is grounds for denial under § 656.24, revocation under § 656.32, debarment under § 656.31(f), or any combination thereof.
§ 656.13
Assurances and obligations of employers.
As part of the Application for Permanent Employment Certification and job offer, the employer seeking to employ an alien must declare, under penalty of perjury, that it has knowledge of and will comply with all the requirements of this part and the conditions of employment set forth in this section. The official or employee declaring compliance for the employer under this part must be the official or employee who is most familiar with the content of and recruitment for the job opportunity and is authorized to provide all information and documentation supporting the Application for Permanent Employment Certification, and to communicate with the Department on behalf of the employer. Each assurance in this section is a material condition of certification, and the employer's declaration of compliance with each is a representation of fact on which the Department relies. A certification is not a substitute for the evidence required by this part, and the CO will not accept a declaration as establishing any fact that the employer is required by this part to document. Failure to attest to compliance with any of the conditions listed below will result in a denial of the Application for Permanent Employment Certification. A false declaration, or a failure to comply with any assurance or obligation in this section, is grounds for denial under § 656.31(a), revocation under § 656.32, and debarment under § 656.31(f); will be referred as provided in § 656.31(b); and is punishable under 18 U.S.C. 1001, 1546, and 1621.
(a) Bona fide job opportunity and non-discrimination.
(1) The job opportunity identified in the Application for Permanent Employment Certification is a bona fide full-time permanent position and the terms and conditions of employment comply with applicable Federal, State, and local laws and regulations.
(2) The job opportunity has been and will continue to be open to any qualified U.S. worker, regardless of race, color, national origin, age, sex, pregnancy, religion, disability, genetic information, or citizenship status, through the date on which the Immigrant Petition is approved by USCIS or the date of determination if the Application for Permanent Employment Certification or Immigrant Petition is denied or withdrawn, whichever is later.
(3) Rejection(s) of any U.S. worker(s) who applied or apply for the job were or will be only for lawful, job-related reasons, and those not rejected on this basis have been or will be hired. The employer has not and will not reject any qualified U.S. worker over the prospective alien based on any cost justifications, including those based on differences in any term, condition, or privileges of employment, including compensation and fringe benefits, between any qualified U.S. worker and the prospective alien. The employer has and will continue to retain records of any hire(s) and rejection(s) as required by this part.
(4) Compliance with anti-discrimination laws. The employer has complied, and will continue to comply, with:
(i) Title VII of the Civil Rights Act of 1964, 42 U.S.C. 2000e et seq., which prohibits discrimination in hiring, discharge, compensation, and the terms, conditions, or privileges of employment because of race, color, religion, sex, or national origin, including discrimination against U.S. workers because of their national origin and in favor of workers of a different national origin, and which is primarily enforced by the EEOC; and
(ii) 8 U.S.C. 1324b, which prohibits discrimination in hiring, recruitment or referral for a fee, and discharge because of citizenship status against protected individuals as defined in 8 U.S.C. 1324b(a)(3) (including U.S. citizens and nationals, lawful permanent residents, refugees, and asylees), prohibits discrimination because of national origin by employers with four or more employees that are not covered by Title VII, prohibits unfair documentary practices in the employment eligibility verification process, and prohibits retaliation, and which is enforced by DOJ.
(iii) Nothing in this part limits any obligation under the laws described in paragraphs (a)(4)(i) and (ii) of this section, and compliance with this part is not a defense to liability under those laws.
(iv) The employer has retained documentation demonstrating that the wage and the terms, conditions, and privileges of employment offered to the alien do not differ from those offered to similarly situated U.S. workers employed in the same or substantially comparable positions within the area of intended employment.
(5) No less favorable terms, conditions, and compensation for U.S. workers. As a condition of certification under 8 U.S.C. 1182(a)(5)(A)(i)(II), and consistent with the obligations described in paragraph (a)(4) of this section, the employer has offered, and will continue to offer, to every able and qualified U.S. worker who applies for, is referred to, or is employed in the job opportunity or in a similarly situated position, terms, conditions, and privileges of employment—including hiring, job assignments, compensation, hours of work, training, fringe benefits, promotion, and demotion—that are no less favorable than those offered to the alien who is the subject of the Application for Permanent Employment ( printed page 74010) Certification This paragraph states a wage-and-working-conditions requirement of the permanent labor certification program; it does not construe, limit, or expand Title VII of the Civil Rights Act of 1964, 8 U.S.C. 1324b, or any other law.
(b) No strike, lockout, or work stoppage. The employer assures that this job opportunity, including all places of employment for which the employer is requesting permanent labor certification, is not vacant because the former occupant(s) is (are) on strike or being locked out in the course of a labor dispute involving a work stoppage.
(c) Offered wage and ability to pay guarantees. The employer has offered, and will continue to offer a wage that:
(1) Equals or exceeds the highest of the applicable Federal, State, or local minimum wage, the PWD for the occupation that is issued by the Department to the employer, as reflected on the Application for Permanent Employment Certification. If, after the issuance of a PWD, the Department issues a new or revised PWD that is assigned to the employer's Application for Permanent Employment Certification, the employer will offer a wage that equals or exceeds the highest of the new prevailing wage, the applicable Federal, State, or local minimum wage, unless notified otherwise by the Department.
(2) Does not and will not result in less favorable treatment in compensation, fringe benefits, or other terms, conditions, and privileges of employment for current or prospective U.S. workers employed in, or seeking to be employed in, similarly situated positions at the employer, as required by all applicable federal anti-discrimination laws and regulations under Title VII of the Civil Rights Act of 1964 and the Immigration and Nationality Act's Antidiscrimination Provision, 8 U.S.C. 1324b.
(3) Is not based on commissions, bonuses, or other incentives, unless the employer guarantees an offered wage earned on a weekly, bi-weekly, or monthly basis that equals or exceeds the prevailing wage.
(4) The employer has the ability to pay with existing, internally-available, non-contingent financial resources owned and controlled by the employer at the time of filing the Application for Permanent Employment Certification for work performed in the job opportunity identified in the Application for Permanent Employment Certification. The employer will be able to place the alien on the employer's payroll on or before the date of application for a visa and admission to the United States.
(d) Good faith recruitment of U.S. workers. The employer has conducted, and will continue to conduct, recruitment of U.S. workers, as required under this part, in good faith, in the same manner, through the same channels, and on terms no less favorable to U.S. workers as those on which it normally recruits and considers U.S. workers in the same job opportunities for which the employer does not seek labor certification to employ an alien(s). This applies to, but is not limited to, the following:
(1) The method, manner, and location of job postings ( e.g., online versus paper job postings; posting in an employer's centralized job web page versus specialized, PERM-specific job web pages) and the duration of any job posting;
(2) The method by which an applicant may apply ( e.g., online via a centralized employer website versus PERM-specific email addresses, fax, or paper applications);
(3) The description of the job opportunity, qualifications, and requirements needed to perform the work, which cannot be tailored to the prospective alien and the requirements for applying cannot be unduly restrictive so as to make it difficult for U.S. workers to be deemed qualified.
(4) Interview requirements and procedures. Employers that wish to require interviews must conduct those interviews by phone or provide an alternative method ( e.g., virtual meetings or conferencing, cloud calls) for the interviews to be conducted in the location where the U.S. worker(s) is/are being recruited so that the worker incurs little or no cost due to the interview. Employers cannot provide a prospective alien with more favorable treatment than U.S. workers with respect to the requirements for applying and conducting interviews for the job opportunity. The employer representative conducting the interview must be a U.S. worker and one who normally interviews or considers, on behalf of the employer, applicants for job opportunities such as that offered the alien which do not involve labor certifications.
(5) Each method by which a U.S. worker may apply for the job opportunity must be operational, monitored, and capable of receiving and retaining applications throughout the period of recruitment, and the employer must retain and submit evidence of its operation as required by § 656.17(g).
(6) Recruitment for the job opportunity through any method, channel, or procedure that is less accessible or less favorable to U.S. workers than the employer's ordinary recruitment for similar job opportunities, or through an application channel that does not meet paragraph (d)(5) of this section, is presumed not to have been conducted in good faith; is an independent ground for denial under § 656.24; and will be referred under § 656.31(b) to DOJ and, where the facts indicate possible discrimination because of national origin, to the EEOC.
(e) Layoffs and consideration of laid-off U.S. workers. With regard to layoffs of any U.S. worker(s) in the same or similar occupation ( i.e., the same SOC code or a substantially comparable position) that is the subject of the Application for Permanent Employment Certification in the area of intended employment of the place(s) of employment, including the placement of the alien with another employer subject to paragraph (f) of this section, the employer:
(1) Must not have laid off any such U.S. worker(s) within 2 years prior to the filing of the application;
(2) If a layoff of any such U.S. worker(s) occurs after filing the application and before the date on which the Immigrant Petition is approved by USCIS, or the date of determination if the Application for Permanent Employment Certification or Immigrant Petition is denied or withdrawn, whichever is later, the employer:
(i) Must notify the NPC, and USCIS in the case of a pending Immigrant Petition, in writing or by any other method specified by the Department in a notice published in the Federal Register or specified by USCIS not later than 5 business days after such layoff(s) occurs; and
(ii) Must offer the job opportunity that is the subject of the Application for Permanent Employment Certification to those laid-off U.S. worker(s), at no less than the offered wage and on the terms and conditions stated in the Application for Permanent Employment Certification, and document whether the U.S. worker(s) refused the job opportunity, was rejected for the job opportunity for lawful, job-related reasons, or was hired.
(f) Placing contract workers with other employers engaging in layoffs prohibited. The employer has not placed, and will not place, the alien who is the subject of the Application for Permanent Employment Certification with another employer (including that employer's parent, subsidiary, or affiliate) where:
(1) There are indicia of an employment relationship between the alien and that other employer, ( printed page 74011) consistent with the criteria set forth in § 655.738(d)(2) of this chapter;
(2) The other employer laid off U.S. workers in its workforce who are employed in the same or similar occupation that is the subject of the Application for Permanent Employment Certification, between the date that is 2 years prior to the filing of the application and the date on which the Immigrant Petition is approved by USCIS or the date of determination if the Application for Permanent Employment Certification or Immigrant Petition is denied or withdrawn, whichever is later.
(g) Compliance with prohibition on improper commerce and employees paying fees. The employer and its agents, attorneys, and/or employees have not offered, and will not seek to offer, for sale, barter or purchase to any individual or entity Applications for Permanent Employment Certification at any time, including applications certified by the Department, and have not sought or received, and will not seek to receive, payment of any kind from the worker(s) for any activity related to obtaining permanent labor certification, including payment of the employer's attorneys' fees, whether as an incentive or inducement to filing, or as a reimbursement for costs incurred in preparing or filing a permanent labor certification application, except when work to be performed by the alien in connection with the job opportunity would benefit or accrue to the person or entity making the payment, based on that person's or entity's established business relationship with the employer. An alien may pay his or her own costs in connection with a labor certification, including attorneys' fees for representation of the alien, except where the same attorney represents both the alien and the employer, such costs must be borne by the employer. Payment includes, but is not limited to, monetary payments; wage concessions, including deductions from wages, salary, or benefits; kickbacks, bribes, or tributes; in kind payments; and free labor.
(h) Compliance with applicable laws. The employer has complied and will continue to comply with applicable Federal, State and local employment-related laws and regulations, including, but not limited to, employment-related health and safety laws, Federal, State, and local anti-discrimination laws, including Title VII of the Civil Rights Act of 1964 (42 U.S.C. 2000e et seq.) and 8 U.S.C. 1324b; anti-trafficking laws, including 18 U.S.C. chapter 77; and all applicable provisions of the Fair Labor Standards Act, 29 U.S.C. 201 et seq. In addition, the employer and its agents and attorneys are prohibited from holding or confiscating workers' passports, visas, or other immigration documents pursuant to 18 U.S.C. 1592(a).
(i) Compliance with record retention requirements. The employer filing an Application for Permanent Employment Certification will retain all documents and records demonstrating compliance with this part for a period of 5 years from the period specified in section 656.10(h).
(j) Cooperation with federal compliance reviews relating to activities conducted under this part. The employer will fully cooperate with any audit, supervised recruitment, compliance review, evaluation, or inspection conducted by DOL and any other federal agency related to compliance with this part.
§ 656.14
Notice of filing.
(a) An employer who desires to hire an alien for permanent employment in the United States must post notice of the filing of the Application for Permanent Employment Certification and be able to document that notice was provided, as follows:
(1) If there is a bargaining representative(s) for the employer's employees in the occupational classification located at the place(s) of employment, the employer must provide written notice, electronically or by mail, by providing a copy of the Application for Permanent Employment Certification and maintaining documentation of where and when it was sent to the bargaining representative(s).
(2) If there is no bargaining representative(s), the employer must post notice in at least two conspicuous locations to all employees located at the place(s) of employment in which the work will be performed by the alien. Electronic posting, such as displaying the notice prominently on any internal or external website that is maintained by the employer, known and readily accessible to all employees, and customarily used by the employer for notices to employees about terms and conditions of employment, is sufficient to meet this posting requirement as long as it otherwise meets the requirements of this section. The notice must be of sufficient size, clearly visible, and unobstructed while posted and must be posted in conspicuous places where the employees of the employer would normally expect to see such employment-related notices and can readily read the posted notice on their way to or from their place of employment. The documentation requirement may be satisfied by providing a copy of the posted notice and stating where and when it was posted, and by providing copies of all the in-house media, whether electronic or print, that were used to distribute notice of the application in accordance with the procedures used for similar positions within the employer's organization.
(b) If the employer intends to employ the alien under the Application for Permanent Employment Certification at a third-party worksite, the employer's obligation to provide notice under paragraph (a) of this section extends to the third-party worksite, regardless of whether the place(s) of employment is/are owned or operated by the employer or by some other person or entity.
(c) In the case of a private household, notice is required under this section only if the household employs one or more U.S. workers at the time the application for labor certification is filed. The documentation requirement may be satisfied by providing a copy of the posted notice to the CO.
(d) The notice of the filing of an Application for Permanent Employment Certification must:
(1) State that notice is being provided in connection with the filing of an Application for Permanent Employment Certification by the employer to sponsor an alien for permanent employment in the job opportunity;
(2) State any person may provide information or documentary evidence bearing on the Application for Permanent Employment Certification to the OFLC Administrator of DOL; and
(3) Provide the physical address of the OFLC Administrator.
(e) The notice must contain the information required for advertisements by § 656.17(e) and contain the information required by paragraph (d) of this section.
(f) The notice of filing must be provided or posted within 10 business days before the Application for Permanent Employment Certification is filed with the NPC and continue to be available or displayed through the date on which the Immigrant Petition is approved by USCIS or the date of determination if the Application for Permanent Employment Certification or Immigrant Petition is denied or withdrawn, whichever is later.
( printed page 74012)
