§ 656.17
Pre-filing recruitment requirements.
(a) Employer obligations. Except for Applications for Permanent Employment Certification involving college or university teachers or professional athletes or professional sports team coaches using the recruitment procedures set forth in § 656.18, an employer must attest to having conducted recruitment under this section prior to the filing of the Application for Permanent Employment Certification and comply with all advertising requirements set forth in paragraphs (b) through (e) of this section.
(b) Required recruitment.
(1) Place a job order with the SWA(s) serving the place(s) of employment.
(2) Where the employer maintains one or more websites for its business operations, place an advertisement for the job opportunity on the web page the employer customarily uses to recruit for job opportunities in the same occupational classification that are not the subject of an Application for Permanent Employment Certification. If the employer does not customarily use its website(s) for its business operations to recruit for job opportunities that are not the subject of an Application for Permanent Employment Certification, the employer must post the job opportunity that is the subject of the Application for Permanent Employment Certification on a web page on all of its business operations website(s) in a conspicuous location.
(3) Place an advertisement for the job opportunity on at least one job search website other than a website maintained by the employer or SWA(s). Where the employer customarily uses one or more job search websites to advertise the job opportunities in the same occupational classification that are not the subject of an Application for Permanent Employment Certification, the employer must place an advertisement for the job opportunity on all such websites using the same method(s) and location(s) that it uses for job opportunities in the same occupational classification that are not the subject of an Application for Permanent Employment Certification.
(4) Where the employer customarily uses one or more social media platforms to advertise job opportunities in the same occupational classification that are not the subject of an Application for Permanent Employment Certification, the employer must place an advertisement for the job opportunity on all such social media platforms using the same method(s) and location(s) that it uses for job opportunities in the same occupational classification that are not the subject of an Application for Permanent Employment Certification.
(5) During the period of time the job order placed with the SWA under paragraph (b)(1) of this section is actively circulating, place an advertisement for the job opportunity in the occupational classification through at least one job fair, on-campus recruiting event, or with a college, university, or trade school's placement office. Where the employer customarily engages in one or more job fairs or on-campus recruiting events or placement offices at different colleges, universities, or trade schools to advertise job opportunities in the same occupational classification that are not the subject of an Application for Permanent Employment Certification, the employer must place an advertisement for the job opportunity in the same occupational classification that is the subject of the Application for Permanent Employment Certification using the same method(s) and location(s) for all such job fairs, on-campus recruitment events, or placement offices that it uses for job opportunities in the same occupational classification that are not the subject of an Application for Permanent Employment Certification.
(c) Length of active recruitment. All advertisements placed in connection with the requirements set forth in paragraphs (b)(1) through (4) of this section must be active for a period of no less than 90 calendar days or the total number of days the employer has customarily advertised for the same or similar job opportunities during the most recent 12 months that were not the subject of an Application for Permanent Employment Certification.
(d) Completion of active recruitment. Each of the advertisements placed in connection with the requirements set forth in paragraphs (b)(1) through (4) of this section must be completed no more than 60 calendar days and no less than 30 calendar days before the expected filing of the Application for Permanent Employment Certification.
(e) Advertising requirements.
(1) All recruitment conducted under this subpart must contain terms and conditions of employment that are not less favorable than those offered to the alien and, at a minimum, must comply with the assurances applicable to the recruitment of U.S. workers set forth in § 656.13. In addition, employers must enable any and all information systems they use for collecting, maintaining, and tracking applicants for non-PERM related recruitment to capture all applications that U.S. workers submit for PERM-related recruitment, and enable all applicant records to remain searchable in the same manner as applicant records for non-PERM related recruitment.
(2) All advertising must contain the following information:
(i) The employer's name and contact information;
(ii) A description of the job opportunity with sufficient information to apprise U.S. workers of the services or labor to be performed, including the duties and actual minimum qualifications or requirements to perform the services or labor and the hours of work per week;
(iii) The place(s) of employment or geographic area of employment, including a statement as to whether the position offers remote work, full-time telework, or a hybrid combination of telework and on-site work, with enough specificity to apprise applicants of any travel requirements and where applicants will likely have to reside to perform the service or labor;
(iv) The wage offer or, in the event that there are multiple wage offers, the range of applicable wage offers, each of which must equal or exceed the highest of the prevailing wage or the Federal, State, or local minimum wage;
(v) If applicable, a statement that overtime will be available to the worker and the wage offer(s) for working any overtime hours; and
(vi) A statement that the job is a full-time position and, if the employer has more than one job opening, indicate the total number of job openings the employer intends to fill.
(3) An employer may recruit for multiple positions simultaneously through a single advertisement, provided that, with respect to each job opportunity for which certification is sought, the multi-position advertisement:
(i) Complies with the pre-filing recruitment timing requirements in paragraph (d) of this section;
(ii) Complies with the recruitment placement and duration requirements in paragraphs (b) and (c) of this section;
(iii) Includes sufficient information to apprise U.S. workers of each job opportunity, including all information required by this paragraph (e).
(f) Recruitment report.
(1) Requirements of a recruitment report. The employer must prepare, sign, and date a written recruitment report. The recruitment report must be completed before it is submitted concurrently with the filing of the Application for Permanent Employment ( printed page 74013) Certification, as set forth in § 656.10, or on a date specified by the CO as required by the procedures for supervised recruitment set forth in § 656.21 and contain the following information:
(i) Identify the name of each recruitment activity, date(s) of advertisement, the uniform resource locator (URL) or other location of each advertisement, and, for each advertisement placed with a website, platform, publisher, or the SWA, the platform- or publisher-generated confirmation number, receipt, or order identifier and the posting and expiration dates it generated;
(ii) State the name and contact information of each U.S. worker, including those laid off by the employer, who applied or was referred to the job opportunity up to the date of the preparation of the recruitment report, categorized by the advertising method through which the U.S. worker(s) applied for the job opportunity; and, for each U.S. workers, specify the date(s) of application, whether the U.S. worker met the actual minimum requirements, the name and title of each employee of the employer who reviewed, screened, or interviewed the applicant, the disposition of each U.S. worker, and whether the job opportunity was offered to the U.S. worker and whether the U.S. worker accepted or declined; and
(iii) If applicable, for each U.S. worker who applied for the job opportunity but was not hired, explain the lawful job-related reason(s) for not hiring the U.S. worker. Where the employer is sponsoring a college or university teacher or an alien with exceptional ability in the sciences or the arts, explain why the alien is more qualified than each U.S. worker who applied.
(2) Duty to update recruitment report. The employer must continue to update the recruitment report whenever it experiences layoffs of any U.S. worker(s) in the same or similar occupation that is the subject of the Application for Permanent Employment Certification in the place(s) of intended employment through the date on which the Immigrant Petition is approved by USCIS or the date of determination if the Application for Permanent Employment Certification or Immigrant Petition is denied or withdrawn, whichever is later. The updated report must be signed and dated by the employer and made available upon request by the CO. The recruitment report update must contain the following information:
(i) For any U.S. worker(s) laid off in the same or similar occupation that is the subject of the Application for Permanent Employment Certification in the place(s) of intended employment, provide a detailed explanation of the method(s) used and date(s) on which the employer notified all potentially qualified laid off U.S. workers for the job opportunity;
(ii) State the name and contact information of each U.S. worker notified and identify each U.S. worker who applied to the job opportunity up to the date of the preparation of the recruitment report, categorized by the notification method through which the U.S. worker(s) applied for the job opportunity, and specifying the disposition of each U.S. worker and whether the job opportunity was offered to the U.S. worker and whether the U.S. worker accepted or declined.
(g) Duty to maintain recruitment documentation. Documentation sufficient to prove compliance with the pre-filing recruitment requirements of this section must be submitted concurrently with the filing of the Application for Permanent Employment Certification, as set forth in 656.10. Documentation sufficient to prove compliance with post-filing notification and consideration of U.S. workers must be retained by the employer in compliance with the requirements of § 656.10(h).
(h) Further recruitment guidance. The OFLC Administrator may from time to time and as necessary provide further clarifying guidance regarding recruitment requirements, methods, and practices. Should it be necessary, the OFLC Administrator will publish the updated guidance on OFLC's website and/or through a notice in the Federal Register .
§ 656.18
Optional pre-filing recruitment requirements for college and university teachers and professional athletes and professional sports team coaches.
(a) Recruitment. Prior to the filing of the Application for Permanent Employment Certification, the employer may conduct recruitment for a job opportunity involving a college or university teacher or a professional athlete or professional sports team coach using the procedures set forth in § 656.17 or must be able to document that the alien was selected for such job opportunity using a competitive recruitment and selection process. For purposes of this section, documentation associated with conducting recruitment using a competitive recruitment and selection process must include:
(1) A written statement, signed and dated by an official who has actual hiring authority from the employer, providing a detailed description of the recruitment procedures undertaken;
(2) A final report or other documentation from the organizational or administrative body making the recommendation or selection of the alien, at the completion of the competitive recruitment and selection process; and
(3) Evidence of all other recruitment sources utilized.
(4) In addition, for a job opportunity involving a college or university teacher:
(i) A copy of at least one print or electronic advertisement for the job opportunity placed in a national professional journal, giving the name and the date(s) of publication; and which states the job title, duties, and requirements; and
(ii) A written statement attesting to the degree(s) of the alien's educational or professional qualifications and academic achievements.
(b) Duty to maintain recruitment documentation. Documentation to prove compliance with the requirements of this section must be submitted concurrently with the filing of the Application for Permanent Employment Certification, as set forth in § 656.10 and retained by the employer in compliance with the requirements of § 656.10(h).
§ 656.19
Processing of an application.
(a) NPC review. The CO will assign for processing all Applications for Permanent Employment Certification, review such applications for compliance with the requirements set forth in this part, and make a decision to conduct an audit examination using the procedures set forth in § 656.20, order supervised recruitment using the procedures set forth in § 656.21, or issue a final determination set forth in § 656.24.
(b) Correspondence during processing. Any notice or request sent by the CO(s) under this subpart to an employer requiring a response will be sent electronically, unless the employer was permitted to file by mail as set forth in § 656.10(c). For employers permitted to file by mail, the CO will send any notice or request via traditional methods to assure next day delivery. The CO will use the address, including electronic mail address, provided on the Application for Permanent Employment Certification to send such notices or requests. The employer's response to such a notice or request must be filed electronically, except that an employer permitted to file by mail as set forth in § 656.10(c), may use traditional methods to assure next day delivery. The employer's response must be sent by the due date or the next business day if the ( printed page 74014) due date falls on a Sunday or Federal holiday.
(c) Response dates. OFLC will determine the date of an employer's response to any notice or request sent to the employer during application processing as follows:
(1) For any Application for Permanent Employment Certification received using the electronic method(s) designated by the OFLC Administrator, response date(s) will be the date on which a response to a notice or request is submitted. Response date(s) will be based on the time of submission in the Eastern time zone of the United States.
(2) For any Application for Permanent Employment Certification received in accordance with § 656.10(c)(2) or (3), response date(s) will be the postmark date affixed to the employer's response submission.
(d) Job duties and requirements.
(1) Job requirements specified in the Application for Permanent Employment Certification, unless adequately documented as arising from business necessity, must be those normally required for the occupation and must not exceed the SVP level assigned to the occupation as shown in the O*NET Job Zones. To establish a business necessity, an employer must demonstrate the job duties and requirements bear a reasonable relationship to the occupation in the context of the employer's business and are essential to perform the job in a reasonable manner.
(2) A foreign language requirement is prohibited, unless such a requirement is justified by business necessity. Demonstrating business necessity for a foreign language requirement may be based upon the following:
(i) The nature of the occupation and the specific duties to be performed in the job opportunity that normally requires regular use of a foreign language; or
(ii) The need to communicate with a large majority of the employer's customers, contractors, or employees who cannot communicate effectively in English, as documented by:
(A) The employer furnishing the number and proportion of its clients, contractors, or employees who cannot communicate effectively in English, and/or detailed executed contracts or agreements outlining plans to market products or deliver services in a foreign country; and
(B) A detailed explanation of why the duties of the job opportunity normally require frequent contact and communication with clients, contractors, or employees who cannot communicate effectively in English, including why the responsibilities of the position for which permanent labor certification is sought necessitate regular contact and communication with these individuals, and why it is reasonable to believe the foreign-language-speaking clients, contractors, and employees cannot communicate effectively in English.
(3) If the job opportunity involves a combination of occupations, the employer must document that it has normally employed workers for that combination of occupations, and/or workers customarily perform the combination of occupations in the place(s) of employment, and/or the combination job opportunity is based on a business necessity. Combination occupations can be documented by position descriptions, relevant payroll records, and/or letters from other employers stating their workers regularly perform the combination of occupations in the same AIE, and/or documentation that the combination occupation arises from a business necessity.
(4)(i) If the job opportunity contains alternative requirements, those requirements must be substantially equivalent to the primary requirements of the job opportunity for which permanent labor certification is sought; and
(ii) If the alien already is employed by the employer, and the alien does not meet the primary job requirements and only potentially qualifies for the job by virtue of the alternative requirements, the CO will deny permanent labor certification unless the Application for Permanent Employment Certification states that any suitable combination of education, training, or experience is acceptable.
(5) If the job opportunity involves a household domestic service worker, live-in requirements are acceptable only if the employer can demonstrate the requirement is essential to perform, in a reasonable manner, the job duties specified in the Application for Permanent Employment Certification and there are not cost-effective alternatives to a live-in household requirement. Mere employer assertions do not constitute acceptable documentation. For example, a live-in requirement could be supported by documenting two working parents and young children in the household, and/or the existence of erratic work schedules requiring frequent travel and a need to entertain business associates and clients on short notice. Depending upon the situation, acceptable documentation could consist of travel vouchers, written estimates of costs of alternatives such as babysitters, or a detailed listing of the frequency and length of absences of the employer from the home.
(e) CO review of job requirements.
(1) The job requirements, as described in the Application for Permanent Employment Certification, must represent only the actual minimum requirements to perform the duties in the job opportunity.
(2) The employer must not have hired workers with less training or experience for jobs substantially comparable to the job opportunity described in the Application for Permanent Employment Certification.
(3) If the alien is already employed by the employer, in considering whether the job requirements represent the actual minimums, the CO will review any training and experience possessed by the alien at the time of hiring by the employer, including as a contract employee. The employer is prohibited from requiring that a U.S. worker(s) possess training and/or experience beyond what the alien possessed at the time of hire unless:
(i) The alien gained the experience while working for the employer, including as a contract employee, in a position not substantially comparable to the position for which permanent labor certification is being sought, or
(ii) The employer can demonstrate that it is no longer feasible to train a worker to qualify for the position.
(4) In evaluating whether the alien satisfies the actual minimum requirements for the job opportunity described in the Application for Permanent Employment Certification, the CO will not consider any education or training obtained by the alien at the employer's expense unless the employer offers similar training to domestic worker applicants.
§ 656.20
Audit.
(a) Discretion. Prior to the issuance of a final determination, the CO may, at their discretion, select an Application for Permanent Employment Certification for audit to determine compliance with the requirements of this part.
(b) Audit notice. Where an Application for Permanent Employment Certification is selected for audit, the CO will issue a Notice of Audit Examination to the employer and a copy, if appropriate, to the employer's agent or attorney. The notice will:
(1) Specify the documentation that must be submitted by the employer;
(2) Specify a date, no more than 30 calendar days from the date the audit notice is issued, by which the required documentation must be sent to the CO; ( printed page 74015)
(3) Advise that if the required documentation has not been sent by the date specified the application will be denied.
(i) Failure to provide documentation in a timely manner constitutes a refusal to exhaust available administrative remedies; and
(ii) The administrative-judicial review procedure provided in § 656.26 is not available.
(4) Advise that failure to fully comply with the audit process will result in a denial of the Application for Permanent Employment Certification under § 656.24, and may result in the imposition of supervised recruitment under § 656.21 for the current and/or future filings of any Application for Permanent Employment Certification for up to 2 years, and/or debarment under § 656.31(f).
(c) One-time extension request. To request an extension, the employer must submit a request in writing to the NPC identifying the Application for Permanent Employment Certification and stating the reason(s) for the extension request. The CO may, in their discretion, provide one extension of up to 30 calendar days, to one response timeframe during audit.
(d) Actions during processing. During the course of the audit examination:
(1) The CO may issue one or more requests for supplemental information and/or documentation from the employer in order to complete the audit, and the CO will specify a timeframe of up to 30 calendar days for responding to any supplemental information request; and/or
(2) The CO may require the employer to conduct supervised recruitment under § 656.21.
(e) Correspondence during audit. Any notice or request sent by the CO(s) under this section to an employer requiring a response will be sent electronically, unless the employer was permitted to file by mail as set forth in § 656.10(c). For employers permitted to file by mail, the CO will send any notice or request via traditional methods to assure next day delivery. The CO will use the address, including electronic mail address, provided on the Application for Permanent Employment Certification to send such notices or requests. The employer's response to such notice or request must be filed electronically, except that an employer permitted to file by mail as set forth in § 656.10(c), may use traditional methods to assure next day delivery. The employer's response must be sent by the due date or the next business day if the due date falls on a Sunday or Federal holiday.
(f) Failure to comply with audit. If the CO determines that an employer failed to timely respond to a notice or request during audit or failed to comply with audit requirements ordered by the CO under this section, the application will be denied. Where the CO determines an employer's audit response is inadequate, the CO will deny the application. The CO may also impose the supervised recruitment procedures under § 656.21 for the employer's future applications for up to 2 years. In addition, the employer and/or its attorney or agent may be debarred consistent with § 656.31(f).
(g) Potential referrals and information sharing. In addition to measures in this subpart, the CO will provide the audit findings for an employer, attorney, or agent and underlying documentation to any enforcement agency with jurisdiction where the findings indicate a possible violation of law within that agency's jurisdiction, may provide such findings and documentation to any enforcement agency in any other case, and will provide such findings and documentation upon the written request of an enforcement agency, including the DOL Office of Inspector General (DOL-OIG), DHS, the Department of Justice (DOJ), including DOJ-IER, and the EEOC.
§ 656.21
Supervised recruitment.
(a) Discretion. The CO may, at their discretion, require supervised recruitment on any Application for Permanent Employment Certification pending a final determination or for all of an employer's future Applications for Permanent Employment Certification for up to 2 years pursuant to § 656.20(f).
(b) Supervised recruitment notice. Where an Application for Permanent Employment Certification is selected for supervised recruitment, the CO will issue a Notice of Supervised Recruitment to the employer and a copy, if appropriate, to the employer's agent or attorney. The notice will:
(1) Specify the procedures for conducting supervised recruitment, including the required method(s) and content for advertising the job opportunity to prospective U.S. applicants ( e.g., whether to have applicants respond directly to the NPC), timeframes for compliance with the supervised recruitment process, and state that supervised recruitment will be incorporated as bona fide conditions and terms of the Application(s) for Permanent Employment Certification.
(2) Advise that if the required documentation has not been sent by the date specified the application will be denied.
(i) Failure to provide documentation in a timely manner constitutes a refusal to exhaust available administrative remedies; and
(ii) The administrative-judicial review procedure provided in § 656.26 is not available.
(c) Requirements. The supervised recruitment process will be in addition to any recruitment required of the employer by this part and may consist of, but is not limited to, one or more of the following:
(1) Requiring the employer to submit one or more draft advertisements to the CO for review and approval prior to conducting recruitment for U.S. workers;
(2) Designating the source(s) where the employer must recruit for U.S. workers and specifying the timeframe(s) in which the employer must place the advertisement(s) in such source(s);
(3) Requiring the employer to notify the CO in writing when the advertisement(s) are placed;
(4) Extending the length of the placement of any advertisement(s) and/or job order placed with the SWA, as necessary, to effectively recruit for U.S. workers;
(5) Requiring the employer to retain proof of publication of all advertisements and provide a copy of any documentation, upon request by the CO, verifying it conducted the recruitment as directed by the CO;
(6) Requiring the employer to submit a recruitment report meeting the requirements under § 656.17(f), including any additional information requested by the CO, within 30 calendar days of the CO's request for such a report; and
(7) Requiring the employer to provide updated notice to employees, as set forth in as set forth in § 656.21, when necessary.
(d) Supplemental information request. During the course of supervised recruitment, the CO may issue one or more requests for supplemental information and/or documentation from the employer in order to complete supervised recruitment. The CO will specify the timeframe of up to 30 calendar days for responding to the supplemental information request.
(e) Correspondence during supervised recruitment. Any notice or request sent by the CO(s) under this section to an employer requiring a response will be sent electronically, unless the employer was permitted to file by mail as set forth in § 656.10(c). For employers permitted to file by mail, the CO will send any notice or request via traditional methods to assure next day delivery. The CO will use the address, including electronic ( printed page 74016) mail address, provided on the Application for Permanent Employment Certification to send such notices or requests. The employer's response to such a notice or request must be filed electronically, except that an employer permitted to file by mail as set forth in § 656.10(c), may use traditional methods to assure next day delivery. The employer's response must be sent by the due date or the next business day if the due date falls on a Sunday or Federal holiday.
(f) Failure to comply with supervised recruitment. If the CO determines that an employer has failed to comply with requirements ordered by the CO under this section, the Application for Permanent Employment Certification will be denied, and the employer and/or its attorney or agent may be debarred consistent with § 656.31(f).
(g) Potential referrals and information sharing. In addition to measures in this subpart, the CO will provide the supervised recruitment findings and underlying documentation to any enforcement agency with jurisdiction where the findings indicate a possible violation of law within that agency's jurisdiction, may provide such findings and documentation to any enforcement agency in any other case, and will provide such findings and documentation upon the written request of an enforcement agency, including the DOL-OIG, DHS, DOJ, including DOJ-IER, and the EEOC.
(h) One-time extension request. To request an extension, the employer must submit a request in writing to the NPC identifying the Application for Permanent Employment Certification and stating the reason(s) for the extension request. The CO may, in their discretion, provide one extension of up to 30 calendar days, to one response timeframe during supervised recruitment.
§ 656.24
Labor certification determinations.
(a) Determinations. The OFLC Administrator is the Department's National CO. The OFLC Administrator and the CO(s), by virtue of delegation from the OFLC Administrator, have the authority to certify or deny Applications for Permanent Employment Certification. The OFLC Administrator has sole discretion to certify, deny, or suspend processing of Applications for Permanent Employment Certification under 656.31(b) or for other appropriate reasons or to delegate authority to the CO to do so.
(b) Criteria for certification. The CO will grant certification only if:
(1) The employer has met all the requirements of this part.
(2) There are not sufficient U.S. workers to fill the job opportunity. In making a determination as to whether there are insufficient U.S. workers to fill the employer's job opportunity, the CO will count as able and available any U.S. worker referred by the SWA or any U.S. worker who applied (or on whose behalf an application is made) directly to the employer, whom the employer has not rejected for a lawful, job-related reason.
(i) The CO must consider a U.S. worker able and qualified for the job opportunity if the worker, by education, training, experience, or a combination thereof, is able to perform in the normally accepted manner the duties involved in the occupation as customarily performed by other U.S. workers similarly employed. For the purposes of this paragraph (b)(2)(i), a U.S. worker is able and qualified for the job opportunity if the worker can acquire the skills necessary to perform the duties involved in the occupation during a reasonable period of on the-job training.
(ii) If the job involves an alien who is a college or university teacher or has exceptional ability in the sciences or the arts, the U.S. worker must be at least as qualified as the alien.
(3) The employment of the alien will not have an adverse effect upon the wages and working conditions of U.S. workers similarly employed. In making this determination, the CO considers such things as: labor market information; the special circumstances of the industry, organization, and/or occupation; the prevailing wage in the place(s) of intended employment; and working conditions, such as hours, that are normal to the occupation and industry.
(c) Approved certification. If permanent labor certification is granted, the CO will send a final determination notice and a copy of the certified Application for Permanent Employment Certification to the employer and a copy, if applicable, to the employer's agent or attorney using an electronic method(s) designated by the OFLC Administrator. For employers permitted to file by mail as set forth in § 656.10(c), the CO will send the final determination notice and a copy of the certified Application for Permanent Employment Certification using the mailing address for the employer identified on the application. The CO will send the certified Application for Permanent Employment Certification directly to USCIS using an electronic method(s) designated by the OFLC Administrator.
(d) Denied certification. If the Application for Permanent Employment Certification is denied, the CO will send a Notice of Denial to the employer and a copy, if appropriate, to the employer's agent or attorney using an electronic method(s) designated by the OFLC Administrator. For employers permitted to file by mail as set forth in § 656.10(c), the CO will send the Notice of Denial by means normally assuring next day delivery. The Notice of Denial will:
(1) State the reason(s) certification is denied, citing the relevant regulatory standards;
(2) Offer the employer an opportunity to request reconsideration by the CO of the denial under § 656.26(a) or request review by BALCA under § 656.26(b);
(3) State that if the employer does not request reconsideration by the CO or review by BALCA within 30 calendar days of the date of the determination, as specified in § 656.26(a) or (b), the denial is final, and the Department will not accept any request for reconsideration or review on that Application for Permanent Employment Certification;
(4) State that if the employer does not request reconsideration or review of the denial under § 656.26(a) or (b), a new Application for Permanent Employment Certification may be filed at any time; and
(5) State that the employer is prohibited from filing a new Application for Permanent Employment Certification in the same occupation for the same alien while a request for reconsideration or review is pending under the procedures set forth in § 656.26 or § 656.27.
§ 656.26
Review of denials of permanent labor certification.
(a) Request for reconsideration by the CO.
(1) The employer may request reconsideration by the CO within 30 calendar days from the date of issuance of the Notice of Denial.
(2) A request for reconsideration may include only:
(i) Documentation that the Department actually received from the employer in response to a request from the CO to the employer; or
(ii) Documentation that the employer did not have an opportunity to present previously to the CO, but that existed at the time the Application for Permanent Employment Certification was filed and maintained by the employer to support the Application for Permanent Employment Certification in compliance with the requirements of § 656.10(h).
(3) Paragraphs (a)(1) and (2) of this section notwithstanding, the CO will not grant any request for reconsideration ( printed page 74017) where the deficiency that caused denial resulted from the applicant's disregard of a system prompt or other direct instruction.
(4) The employer's request must clearly state whether it is seeking a request for review by BALCA or a request for reconsideration by the CO. If it is not clearly stated whether the employer is requesting reconsideration by the CO or requesting review by BALCA, the CO will reconsider the determination.
(5) If the employer requests reconsideration, the CO will reconsider the Application for Permanent Employment Certification. If the reconsideration is denied, the Notice of Reconsideration Denial will:
(i) State the reasons for the Notice of Reconsideration Denial;
(ii) Quote the request for review procedures at paragraphs (b) and (c) of this section;
(iii) Advise that failure to request review by BALCA of the Notice of Reconsideration Denial within 30 calendar days of the date of determination constitutes a failure to exhaust administrative remedies;
(iv) Advise that, if a request for review is not made within 30 calendar days of the determination, the Notice of Reconsideration Denial will become the final determination of the Secretary;
(v) Advise that if the reconsideration is denied, and a request for review is not made in accordance with the procedures at paragraph (b) of this section, a new application may be filed at any time; and
(vi) Advise that a new application in the same occupation for the same alien cannot be filed while a request for review is pending with BALCA.
(b) Request for review by BALCA.
(1) If a permanent labor certification is denied under § 656.24(d), if a request for reconsideration is denied under paragraph (a) of this section, if a permanent labor certification is revoked pursuant to § 656.32, or if a debarment is issued under § 656.31(f), a request for review of the denial of certification, denial on reconsideration, revocation, or debarment may be made to BALCA by the employer or debarred person or entity by making a request for such an administrative review in accordance with the procedures provided in this paragraph (b). In the case of a finding of debarment, receipt by the Department of a request for review, if made in accordance with this section, will stay the debarment until such time as the review has been completed and a decision rendered thereon.
(2) A request for review of a denial of certification, denial on reconsideration, or revocation:
(i) Must be sent within 30 calendar days of the date of the determination to the CO who denied the application, denied the request for reconsideration, or revoked the certification;
(ii) Must clearly identify the particular labor certification determination for which review is sought;
(iii) Must set forth the particular grounds for the request; and
(iv) Must include a copy of the final determination.
(3) A request for review of debarment:
(i) Must be sent to the OFLC Administrator within 30 calendar days of the date of the debarment determination;
(ii) Must clearly identify the particular debarment determination for which review is sought;
(iii) Must set forth the particular grounds for the request; and
(iv) Must include a copy of the Notice of Debarment.
(4)(i) With respect to a request for review of either a Notice of Denial or a Notice of Reconsideration Denial, statements, briefs, and other submissions of the parties and amicus curiae must contain only legal argument and only such evidence as was actually before the CO at the time of the CO's decision, even if such evidence is in the administrative file.
(ii) With respect to a revocation or a debarment determination, BALCA proceeding may be de novo.
(c) Denial of certification, denial on reconsideration, or revocation Appeal File. Upon the receipt of a request for review, the CO immediately must assemble an indexed Appeal File:
(1) The Appeal File must be in chronological order, must have the index on top followed by the most recent document, and must have consecutively numbered pages. The Appeal File must contain the request for review, the complete application file, and copies of all the written material, such as pertinent parts and pages of surveys and/or reports upon which the denial was based.
(2) The CO must send the Appeal File to BALCA, Office of Administrative Law Judges, the employer, the employer's attorney or agent (if applicable), and the Associate Solicitor for Employment and Training Legal Services, Office of the Solicitor, U.S. DOL (counsel), as soon as practicable.
(3) The employer may furnish or suggest directly to BALCA the addition of any documentation that is not in the Appeal File, but was actually before the CO at the time of the CO's decision. The employer must submit such documentation in writing, and must send a copy to the Associate Solicitor for Employment and Training Legal Services, Office of the Solicitor, U.S. Department of Labor, Washington, DC 20210.
(d) Debarment Appeal File. Upon the receipt of a request for review of debarment, the OFLC Administrator, immediately must assemble an indexed Appeal File:
(1) The Appeal File must be in chronological order, must have the index on top followed by the most recent document, and must have consecutively numbered pages. The Appeal File must contain the request for review, the complete application file, and copies of all the written material, such as pertinent parts and pages of surveys and/or reports upon which the denial was based.
(2) The OFLC Administrator must send the Appeal File to BALCA, Office of Administrative Law Judges, the employer, the employer's attorney or agent (if applicable), and the Associate Solicitor for Employment and Training Legal Services, Office of the Solicitor, U.S. DOL (counsel), by means normally assuring next-day delivery.
(3) The debarred person or entity may furnish or suggest directly to BALCA the addition of any documentation in writing that is not in the Appeal File. The debarred person or entity must submit such documentation in writing, and must send a copy to the Associate Solicitor for Employment and Training Legal Services, Office of the Solicitor, U.S. Department of Labor.
§ 656.27
Consideration by and decisions of the Board of Alien Labor Certification Appeals.
(a) Panel designations. In considering requests for review before it, BALCA may sit in panels of three members. The Chief ALJ may designate any BALCA member to submit proposed findings and recommendations to BALCA or to any duly designated panel thereof to consider a particular case.
(b) Briefs and Statements of Position. In considering the requests for review before it, BALCA must afford all parties 30 calendar days to submit or decline to submit any appropriate Statement of Position or legal brief. The CO is to be represented solely by the Solicitor of Labor or the Solicitor's designated representative.
(c) Standard of Review. When reviewing requests for review of denial of permanent labor certification under § 656.24(d), a denial of reconsideration under § 656.26(a), or an affirmation of a ( printed page 74018) PWD under § 656.41, BALCA must uphold the CO's decision unless shown by the employer to be arbitrary, capricious, an abuse of discretion, or otherwise not in accordance with the law.
(d) Review on the record. BALCA must review a denial of permanent labor certification under § 656.24(d), a denial of reconsideration under § 656.26(a), a revocation of a certification under § 656.32, or an affirmation of a PWD under § 656.41 on the basis of the record upon which the CO's final determination was made, the request for review, and any Statements of Position or legal briefs submitted and, except in cases over which the Secretary has assumed jurisdiction pursuant to 29 CFR 18.95, must:
(1) Affirm the denial of the permanent labor certification, the denial of the request for reconsideration, the revocation of certification, or the affirmation of the PWD;
(2) Direct the CO to grant the certification only when the CO had an opportunity to review the merits of the application, overrule the revocation of certification, or overrule the affirmation of the PWD;
(3) Remand to the CO for further action; or
(4) Direct that a hearing on the case be held under paragraph (f) of this section.
(e) Notifications of decisions. BALCA must notify the employer, employer's attorney or agent (if applicable), the CO, and the Solicitor of Labor of its decision, and must return the record to the CO unless the case has been set for hearing under paragraph (f) of this section.
(f) Hearings.
(1) Notification of hearing. If the case has been set for a hearing, BALCA must notify the employer, the employer's attorney or agent (if applicable), the alien, the CO, and the Solicitor of Labor of the date, time, and place of the hearing, and that the hearing may be rescheduled upon written request.
(2) Hearing procedure.
(i) The “Rules of Practice and Procedure for Administrative Hearings Before the Office of Administrative Law Judges,” at 29 CFR part 18, apply to hearings under this paragraph (f).
(ii) For the purposes of this paragraph (f)(2), references in 29 CFR part 18 to: “administrative law judge” mean the BALCA member or the BALCA panel duly designated under paragraph (a) of this section; “Office of Administrative Law Judges” means BALCA; and “Chief Administrative Law Judge” means the Chief ALJ in that official's function of chairing BALCA.
§ 656.30
Validity of labor certifications.
(a) Validity period. A permanent labor certification is only valid when filed in support of an initial Immigrant Petition with USCIS within 180 calendar days from the date on which the Application for Permanent Employment Certification is certified.
(b) Scope of validity. A permanent labor certification is valid only for the job opportunity, the alien, the place(s) of intended employment, and the employer specified on the certified Application for Permanent Employment Certification, including all appendices and any modifications approved by the Department. The permanent labor certification may not be transferred from one employer to another unless the employer to which it is transferred is a successor in interest to the employer to which it was issued.
§ 656.31
Applications involving fraud, willful misrepresentation, or violations of this part.
(a) Denial. The CO will deny any Application for Permanent Employment Certification under § 656.24 where the CO determines that the application contains false statements of material fact, is fraudulent, contains willful misrepresentations, or was otherwise submitted in violation of the requirements of this part.
(b) Possible fraud or willful misrepresentation.
(1) If the Department learns an employer, attorney, or agent is involved in possible fraud or willful misrepresentation in connection with the permanent labor certification program, the Department will refer the matter to DOJ, DHS, or other government entity, as appropriate, for investigation, and send a copy of the referral to the DOL-OIG. If the Department learns an employer, attorney, or agent is involved in possible discrimination because of national origin, the Department will also refer the matter to the EEOC; where it indicates possible discrimination because of citizenship status, or because of national origin by an employer not covered by Title VII, the Department will also refer the matter to DOJ. In these cases, or if the Department learns an employer, attorney, or agent is under investigation by DOJ, DHS, or other government entity for possible fraud or willful misrepresentation in connection with the permanent labor certification program, the Department may suspend processing of any Application for Permanent Employment Certification involving such employer, attorney, or agent until completion of any investigation and/or judicial proceedings. Unless the investigatory agency, in writing, requests the Department to do otherwise, the Department will provide written notification to the employer and a copy, if appropriate, to the employer's agent or attorney of the suspension in processing.
(2) A suspension pursuant to paragraph (b)(1) of this section may last initially for up to 180 calendar days. No later than 180 calendar days after the suspension began, if no criminal indictment or information has been issued, or judicial proceedings have not been concluded, the CO may resume processing any of the Applications for Permanent Employment Certification, or may extend the suspension in processing until completion of any investigation and/or judicial proceedings.
(c) Criminal indictment or information. If the Department learns that an employer, attorney, or agent is named in a criminal indictment or information in connection with the permanent labor certification program, the processing of any of the Applications for Permanent Employment Certification related to that employer, attorney, or agent may be suspended until the judicial process is completed. Unless the investigatory or prosecutorial agency, in writing, requests the Department to do otherwise, the Department will provide written notification to the employer and a copy, if appropriate, to the employer's agent or attorney of the suspension in processing.
(d) No finding of fraud or willful misrepresentation. If an employer, attorney, or agent is acquitted of fraud or willful misrepresentation charges, or if such criminal charges are withdrawn or otherwise fail to result in a finding of fraud or willful misrepresentation, the CO will resume processing of the Applications for Permanent Employment Certification in accordance with the requirements of this part based on the merits of the application.
(e) Finding of fraud or willful misrepresentation. If an employer, attorney, or agent is found to have committed fraud or willful misrepresentation involving the permanent labor certification program, whether by a court, the Department of State or DHS, as referenced in § 656.32(c), by the OFLC Administrator under paragraph (f)(3) of this section, or through other proceedings:
(1) Any suspension of processing of pending Applications for Permanent Employment Certification related to that employer, attorney, or agent will terminate. ( printed page 74019)
(2) The CO will decide each such Application for Permanent Employment Certification on its merits and may deny any such Application for Permanent Employment Certification under § 656.24 and paragraph (a) of this section.
(3) In the case of a pending Application for Permanent Employment Certification involving an attorney or agent found to have committed fraud or willful misrepresentation, the Department will notify the employer associated with that Application for Permanent Employment Certification of the finding and require the employer to notify the Department in writing, within 30 calendar days of the notification, whether the employer will withdraw the Application for Permanent Employment Certification, designate a new attorney or agent, or continue the Application for Permanent Employment Certification without representation. Failure of the employer to respond within 30 calendar days of the notification will result in a denial under § 656.24. If the employer elects to continue representation by the attorney or agent, DOL will suspend processing of any affected Application for Permanent Employment Certification while debarment proceedings are conducted under paragraph (f) of this section.
(f) Debarment.
(1) No later than 6 years after the date of filing of the Application for Permanent Employment Certification that is the basis for the finding, or, if such basis requires a pattern or practice as provided in paragraphs (f)(1)(iii) through (viii) of this section, no later than 6 years after the date of filing of the last Application for Permanent Employment Certification which constitutes a part of the pattern or practice, the OFLC Administrator may issue to an employer, attorney, agent, or any combination thereof a Notice of Debarment for not more than 3 years, based upon any action that was prohibited at the time the action occurred, upon determining the employer, attorney, or agent has participated in or facilitated one or more of the following:
(i) The sale, barter, or purchase of any Application for Permanent Employment Certification, or any other action prohibited under § 656.12;
(ii) The willful provision or willful assistance in the provision of false or inaccurate information in applying for permanent labor certification;
(iii) A pattern or practice of a failure to comply with the terms of the Application for Permanent Employment Certification;
(iv) A pattern or practice of failure to comply in the audit process pursuant to § 656.20;
(v) A pattern or practice of failure to comply in the supervised recruitment process pursuant to § 656.21;
(vi) Conduct resulting in a determination by a court, DHS, DOS, or the OFLC Administrator under paragraph (f)(3) of this section of fraud or willful misrepresentation involving an Application for Permanent Employment Certification, as referenced in paragraph (e) of this section;
(vii) A pattern or practice, by an attorney or agent, of preparing or submitting Applications for Permanent Employment Certification on behalf of two or more unrelated employers that contain materially the same job duties, requirements, or recruitment procedures where the CO has determined that those requirements were not the employers' actual minimum requirements or that the recruitment was not conducted in good faith; or
(viii) Failure to pay the alien or a U.S. worker hired for the job opportunity the offered wage stated in the certified Application for Permanent Employment Certification.
(2) The Notice of Debarment will be in writing; will state the reason for the debarment finding, including a detailed explanation of how the employer, attorney or agent has participated in or facilitated one or more of the actions listed in paragraphs (f)(1)(i) through (viii) of this section; will state the start date and term of the debarment; will state that the employer, attorney, or agent will be debarred from all foreign labor certification programs ( i.e., disqualified from filing any labor certification applications or labor condition applications or receiving approvals from OFLC) for the term of the debarment; and will identify appeal opportunities under § 656.26. The debarment will take effect on the start date identified in the Notice of Debarment unless a request for review is filed within the time permitted by § 656.26. DOL will notify DHS and the Department of State regarding any Notice of Debarment.
(3) Independent Department finding. The OFLC Administrator may determine that an employer, attorney, or agent has committed fraud or willful misrepresentation, as defined in 656.3, in connection with an Application for Permanent Employment Certification. Such a determination does not require, and is not contingent upon, any determination by a court, DHS, or the Department of State, and does not preclude a referral under paragraph (b) of this section. A Notice of Debarment issued on the basis of such a determination will set forth the evidence relied upon, and the employer, attorney, or agent may seek review under 656.26.
(g) False statements. To knowingly and willfully furnish any false information in the preparation of the Application for Permanent Employment Certification and any supporting documentation, or to aid, abet, or counsel another to do so is a Federal offense, punishable by fine or imprisonment up to five years, or both under 18 U.S.C. 2 and 1001. Other penalties apply as well to fraud or misuse of DOL immigration-related documents and to perjury with respect to such documents under 18 U.S.C. 1546 and 1621.
§ 656.32
Revocation and invalidation of permanent labor certifications.
(a) Basis for DOL revocation. The OFLC Administrator may revoke a permanent labor certification issued under this part, if the certification was not justified.
(b) DOL procedures for revocation.
(1) The OFLC Administrator sends to the employer and a copy, if appropriate, to the employer's agent or attorney a Notice of Intent to Revoke an approved permanent labor certification which contains a detailed statement of the grounds for the revocation and the time period allowed for the employer's rebuttal. The employer may submit evidence in rebuttal within 30 calendar days of receipt of the notice. The OFLC Administrator must consider all relevant evidence presented in deciding whether to revoke the permanent labor certification.
(2) If rebuttal evidence is not filed by the employer, the Notice of Intent to Revoke becomes the final decision of the Secretary.
(3) If the employer files rebuttal evidence and the OFLC Administrator determines the approved permanent labor certification should be revoked, the employer may file an appeal under § 656.26.
(4) The OFLC Administrator will inform the employer within 30 calendar days of receiving any rebuttal evidence whether or not the approved permanent labor certification will be revoked.
(5) If the approved permanent labor certification is revoked, the OFLC Administrator will also send a copy of the notification to DHS and DOS.
(c) DHS or DOS invalidation of labor certifications. Either DHS or a Consul of the DOS may invalidate a permanent labor certification approved under this part upon a determination, made in accordance with those agencies' ( printed page 74020) procedures or by a court, of fraud or willful misrepresentation of a material fact involving the permanent labor certification application. If evidence of such fraud or willful misrepresentation becomes known to the OFLC Administrator, the OFLC Administrator will notify DHS or DOS, in writing. The OFLC Administrator will also send a copy of the notification to the regional or national office, as appropriate, of the DOL-OIG.
5. Revise subpart D to read as follows:
Subpart D—Determination of Prevailing Wage
656.40
Determination of prevailing wage for labor certification purposes.
656.41
Review of prevailing wage determinations.
§ 656.40
Determination of prevailing wage for labor certification purposes.
(a) Application filing requirements. Except as otherwise permitted by this part, an employer who desires to file an Application for Permanent Employment Certification to hire an alien for permanent employment in the United States must obtain a PWD from the NPC designated by the OFLC Administrator. This section provides the procedures employers must follow when filing.
(1) What to file.
(i) An employer that desires to apply for permanent labor certification of an immigrant worker must file a completed Application for Prevailing Wage Determination, including any relevant appendices and supporting documentation, to obtain a PWD from the NPC.
(ii) The Application for Prevailing Wage Determination must include the employer's legal name, trade name(s), and a valid FEIN as well as a valid place of business (physical location) in the United States.
(iii) If an employer is requesting a PWD based on a CBA covering the job opportunity, the employer must concurrently submit with its Application for Prevailing Wage Determination at the time of filing the following:
(A) A copy of the relevant sections of the CBA, which must include the validity dates of the agreement; the position(s) and description of the job(s) covered; the wage section, including tables and explanations; and the signature page with effective dates of the agreement;
(B) A letter on letterhead from the employer or employer's representative that states the relevant portions of the agreement with effective dates; the title of the position in the agreement appropriate to the job duties, requirements, and location; and wage value appropriate to the job duties, requirements, and location; and
(C) A letter on letterhead from the CBA authorized representative that states the relevant portions of the agreement with effective dates; the title of the position in the agreement appropriate to the job duties, requirements, and location; and wage value appropriate to the job duties, requirements, and location.
(iv) If an employer is requesting a PWD for a job opportunity for a professional athlete or professional sports team coach covered by professional sports league rules or regulations with wage provisions, the employer must concurrently submit with its Application for Prevailing Wage Determination at the time of filing a copy of the applicable league rules or regulations.
(v) If an employer is requesting a PWD based on an employer-provided survey under paragraph (f) of this section, the employer must concurrently submit with its Application for Prevailing Wage Determination at the time of filing a copy of the survey containing specific information about the survey methodology, including sample size and source, sample selection procedures, survey job descriptions, job and wage leveling chart, and list of participants surveyed, to allow a determination of whether the survey meets all the requirements of this section.
(2) Location and method of filing.
(i) Electronic filing. The employer must file the Application for Prevailing Wage Determination, including any relevant appendices, with the NPC using the electronic method(s) designated by the OFLC Administrator. The NPC will return without review any application submitted using a method other than the designated electronic method(s), unless the employer submits the application in accordance with paragraph (a)(2)(ii) of this section.
(ii) Filing by mail. Employers that lack adequate access to electronic filing may file the Application for Prevailing Wage Determination by mail in accordance with the procedures set forth in § 656.10(c)(2) and (3).
(b) NPC review.
(1) The CO will review the Application for Prevailing Wage Determination for compliance with all applicable program requirements and make a decision to issue a Request for Information (RFI) under paragraph (c) of this section or a PWD.
(2) Any notice or request sent by the CO(s) to an employer requiring a response will be sent electronically, unless the employer was permitted to file by mail as set forth in § 656.10(c). For employers permitted to file by mail, the CO will send any notice or request via traditional methods to assure next day delivery. The CO will use the address, including electronic mail address, provided on the Application for Prevailing Wage Determination to send such notices or requests. The employer's response to such a notice or request must be filed electronically, except that an employer permitted to file by mail as set forth in § 656.10(c), may use traditional methods to assure next day delivery. The employer's response must be sent by the due date or the next business day if the due date falls on a Sunday or Federal holiday.
(3) The CO will return without review any Application for Prevailing Wage Determination where a valid PWD has been issued for the same employer covering the same job opportunity and AIE(s) for the same wage year, including applications where the employer has requested review of a PWD under § 656.41.
(c) Request for Information.
(1) If the CO determines the Application for Prevailing Wage Determination is incomplete, contains errors or inaccuracies, or otherwise contains information that is not sufficient to issue a PWD, the CO will issue an RFI to the employer and to the employer's attorney or agent (if applicable). The CO may issue one or more additional RFIs before issuing a final decision.
(2) The RFI will:
(i) State the reason(s) the Application for Prevailing Wage Determination does not meet the criteria for issuing a PWD;
(ii) Offer the employer an opportunity to respond to the RFI within 7 calendar days from the date the RFI was issued by CO;
(iii) State that the CO's determination on whether to issue a PWD will be made based on a review of the employer's response and the CO will make the necessary amendments to the Application for Prevailing Wage Determination to issue a PWD, as applicable; and
(iv) State that if the employer does not respond to the RFI within 7 calendar days from the date the RFI was issued by the CO, the Application for Prevailing Wage Determination will be deemed abandoned, the CO will return the application to the employer without issuing a PWD, and the employer may file a new Application for Prevailing Wage Determination with the NPC.
(d) Determinations. Prevailing wages will be determined as follows: ( printed page 74021)
(1) If the job opportunity is covered by CBA that was negotiated at arms-length between the union and the employer or by professional sports league rules or regulations with wage provisions, the wage rate set forth in the CBA agreement or professional sports league rules or regulations is considered as not adversely affecting the wages of U.S. workers similarly employed, that is, it is considered the “prevailing wage” for labor certification purposes.
(2) If the job opportunity is not covered by a CBA or professional sports league rules or regulations, the prevailing wage for labor certification purposes will be the arithmetic mean, of the wages of workers similarly employed in the AIE, except as provided in paragraph (e) or (f) of this section. The wage component of the Occupational Employment and Wage Statistics Survey will be used to determine the arithmetic mean, unless the employer provides an acceptable survey under paragraph (f) of this section.
(3) If the employer provides a survey acceptable under paragraph (f) of this section, the survey must provide an arithmetic mean of the wages of workers similarly employed in the AIE.
(4) The employer may utilize a current wage determination in the area under the Davis-Bacon Act, 40 U.S.C. 276a et seq.,29 CFR part 1, or the McNamara-O'Hara Service Contract Act, 41 U.S.C. 351 et seq.
(e) Institutions of higher education and research entities. In computing the prevailing wage for a job opportunity in an occupational classification in an AIE for an employee of an institution of higher education, or an affiliated or related nonprofit entity, a nonprofit research organization, or a Governmental research organization, the prevailing wage level takes into account the wage levels of employees only at such institutions and organizations in the AIE.
(1) The organizations listed in this paragraph (e) are defined by DHS at 8 CFR 214.2(h)(19)(iii) as follows:
(i) Institution of higher education means an institution of higher education as defined in section 101(a) of the Higher Education Act of 1965;
(ii) Affiliated or related nonprofit entity means a nonprofit entity that is related or affiliated with an institution of higher education, if it satisfies any of the following conditions:
(A) The nonprofit entity is connected to or associated with an institution of higher education through shared ownership or control by the same board or federation;
(B) The nonprofit entity is operated by an institution of higher education;
(C) The nonprofit entity is attached to an institution of higher education as a member, branch, cooperative, or subsidiary; or
(D) The nonprofit entity has entered into a formal written affiliation agreement with an institution of higher education that establishes an active working relationship between the nonprofit entity and the institution of higher education for the purposes of research or education, and a fundamental activity of the nonprofit entity is to directly contribute to the research or education mission of the institution of higher education. A nonprofit entity may engage in more than one fundamental activity.
(iii) Nonprofit research organization or Governmental research organization. When a fundamental activity of a nonprofit organization is engaging in basic research and/or applied research, that organization is a nonprofit research organization. When a fundamental activity of a Governmental organization is the performance or promotion of basic research and/or applied research, that organization is a government research organization. A Governmental research organization may be a Federal, State, or local entity. A nonprofit research organization or governmental research organization may perform or promote more than one fundamental activity. Basic research is general research to gain more comprehensive knowledge or understanding of the subject under study, without specific applications in mind. Basic research is also research that advances scientific knowledge but does not have specific immediate commercial objectives although it may be in fields of present or potential commercial interest. Applied research is research to gain knowledge or understanding to determine the means by which a specific, recognized need may be met. Applied research includes investigations oriented to discovering new scientific knowledge that has specific commercial objectives with respect to products, processes, or services. Both basic research and applied research may include research and investigation in the sciences, social sciences, or humanities and may include designing, analyzing, and directing the research of others if on an ongoing basis and throughout the research cycle.
(2) Nonprofit organization or entity, for the purpose of this paragraph (e), means an organization qualified as a tax exempt organization under the Internal Revenue Code of 1986, section 501(c)(3), (c)(4), or (c)(6) (26 U.S.C. 501(c)(3), (c)(4) or (c)(6)), and which has received approval as a tax exempt organization from the Internal Revenue Service, as it relates to research or educational purposes.
(f) Employer-provided wage information.
(1) If the job opportunity is not covered by a CBA, or by a professional sports league's rules or regulations, the CO will consider wage information provided by the employer in making a PWD.
(2) The survey submitted to the NPC must be based upon recently collected data.
(i) A published survey must have been published within 24 months of the date of submission to the NPC, must be the most current edition of the survey, and the data upon which the survey is based must have been collected within 24 months of the publication date of the survey.
(ii) A survey conducted by the employer must be based on data collected within 24 months of the date it is submitted to the NPC.
(g) Similarly employed. For purposes of this section, similarly employed means having substantially comparable jobs in the occupational category in the AIE, except that, if a representative sample of workers in the occupational category cannot be obtained in the AIE, similarly employed means:
(1) Having jobs requiring a substantially similar level of skills within the AIE; or
(2) If there are no substantially comparable jobs in the AIE, having substantially comparable jobs with employers outside of the AIE.
(h) Validity of the PWD.
(1) The CO must specify the validity period of the prevailing wage, which in no event may be less than 90 calendar days or more than 365 calendar days from the determination date. A request for review under § 656.41 renders the PWD invalid until the CO issues an affirmed or modified PWD.
(2) An employer must begin the recruitment of U.S. workers required by §§ 656.17, 656.18, or 656.21 within the validity period specified on the Application for Prevailing Wage Determination.
(3) Where the Application for Prevailing Wage Determination contains multiple AIEs covering the same job opportunity, the PWD issued by the CO on such an application may be used in connection with the filing of multiple Applications for Permanent Employment Certification under § 656.10.
(i) Withdrawal of an Application for Prevailing Wage Determination. The ( printed page 74022) employer may withdraw an Application for Prevailing Wage Determination before the CO issues a PWD under this section by submitting a request in writing, electronically or, if permitted to file by mail, via mail, to the NPC that identifies the unique application number and states the reason(s) for the withdrawal.
§ 656.41
Review of prevailing wage determinations.
(a) Review of NPC PWD. Any employer desiring review of a PWD made by a CO must make a request for such review within 30 calendar days of the date from when the PWD was issued. The request for review must be sent to the director of the NPC that issued the PWD within 30 calendar days of the date of the PWD; clearly identify the PWD from which review is sought; set forth the particular grounds for the request; and include all the materials pertaining to the PWD submitted to the NPC up to the date of the PWD received from the NPC.
(b) Processing of request by NPC. Upon the receipt of a request for review, the NPC will review the employer's request and accompanying documentation and add any material that may have been omitted by the employer, including any material the NPC sent the employer up to the date of the PWD.
(c) Review on the record. The director will review the PWD solely on the basis upon which the PWD was made and, upon the request for review, may either affirm or modify the PWD.
(d) Request for review by BALCA. Any employer desiring review of the director's determination must make a request for review by BALCA within 30 calendar days of the date of the Director's decision.
(1) The request for review, statements, briefs, and other submissions of the parties and amicus curiae must contain only legal arguments and only such evidence that was within the record upon which the director made his/her affirmation of the PWD.
(2) The request for review must be in writing and addressed to the director of the NPC making the determination. Upon receipt of a request for a review, the director will assemble an indexed appeal file in reverse chronological order, with the index on top followed by the most recent document.
(3) The director will send the Appeal File to the Office of Administrative Law Judges, BALCA. BALCA handles the appeals in accordance with §§ 656.26 and 656.27.
6. Add a new subpart E to read as follows:
Subpart E—Severability
656.50
Severability.
§ 656.50
Severability.
If any provision of this part is held to be invalid or unenforceable by its terms, or as applied to any person or circumstance, or stayed pending further agency action, the provision will be construed so as to continue to give the maximum effect to the provision permitted by law, unless such holding is one of total invalidity or unenforceability, in which event the provision will be severable from this part and will not affect the remainder thereof.
Marek Laco,
Acting Assistant Secretary for Employment and Training, Labor.

3. See 8 U.S.C. 1153(b)(2)-(3), 1182(a)(5)(D). Section 1153(b)(2) governs the EB-2 classification of immigrant visas granted to aliens who are either professionals holding advanced degrees (master's degree or above or foreign equivalent degree, or a bachelor degree or foreign equivalent degree plus five years of progressive experience in the specialty) or persons of “exceptional ability” in the sciences, arts, or business. To gain entry in this category, the alien must have a prearranged offer of employment with a U.S. employer that meets the requirements of labor certification, without exception.

155. The Department has repeatedly recognized its inherent suspension and debarment authority in the foreign labor certification context. As the Second Circuit found in Janik Paving & Construction, Inc. v. Brock, 828 F.2d 84 (2d Cir. 1987), the Department possesses an inherent authority to refuse to provide a benefit or lift a restriction for an employer that has acted contrary to the welfare of U.S. workers. In assessing the Department's authority to debar violators, the court found that “[t]he Secretary may . . . make such rules and regulations allowing reasonable variations, tolerances, and exemptions to and from any or all provisions . . . as he may find necessary and proper in the public interest to prevent injustice or undue hardship or to avoid serious impairment of the conduct of Government business.” Id. at 89 n.6. In that case, the implied authority to debar existed even though the statute in question ”specifically provided civil and criminal sanctions for violations of overtime work requirements but failed to mention debarment.” Id. at 89. The court held that debarment may be necessary to “effective enforcement of a statute.” Id. at 91; see also Temporary Non-Agricultural Employment of H-2B Aliens in the United States,80 FR 24083 (Apr. 29, 2015) (discussing debarment under 20 CFR 655.73(i)), and Labor Certification Process for Temporary Employment in the Commonwealth of the Northen Mariana Islands (CW-1 Workers),84 FR 12412-12414 (Apr. 1, 2019) (discussing debarment under 20 CFR 655.473(b)).

See Bureau of Labor Statistics, History: Handbook of Methods: U.S. Bureau of Labor Statistics,” available at https://www.bls.gov/​opub/​hom/​oews/​history.htm (last accessed Mar. 3, 2026). The Department will continue to issue PWDs based on “occupational categories based on the Office of Management and Budget's 2018 Standard Occupational Classification (SOC) system.” Occupational Employment and Wage Statistics, Technical Notes for May 2025 OEWS Estimates (last updated May 15, 2026), https://www.bls.gov/​oes/​2025/​may/​oes_​tec.htm. The SOC system is the authoritative resource for occupational definitions within the United States. Of note, the O*NET OnLine, an oft used tool by stakeholders in prevailing wage determination applications, is an application for the “general public” based on the SOC system but is not the authoritative source for defining occupations. National Center for O*NET Development, O*NET OnLine Help, OnLine Overview, O*NET OnLine (last updated May 19, 2026), https://www.onetonline.org/​help/​online/​.
